The Airline That Invented Premium Economy Just Built A 42-Inch Seat Nobody Else Can

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Getting passengers in seats is the number one priority of all airlines. How an airline achieves this is usually down to routes as well as the product itself and how it differs from others in the market. Today, the most contested part of the cabin has arguably become premium economy. Global carriers are racing to capture affluent long-haul commuters, and the strategic challenge is no longer just about building a spacious seat, but deploying it where it can inflict the maximum competitive damage. EVA Air is one of the airlines that is leading the way, a pioneer of the premium economy concept, and still retaining its dominance.

The airline's recent progress signals a deeper structural shift in how boutique international airlines challenge massive legacy alliances on ultra-long-haul flight tracks. Instead of funneling travelers through crowded West Coast transit points, the focus has shifted toward capturing high-yield corporate and premium leisure demand directly from prime East Coast markets.

First, the new staple route between Taipei Taoyuan International Airport (TPE) and Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD) begins a tale of aggressive expansion of intercontinental capacity. Operating four times per week, this new corridor marks the carrier's tenth North American gateway, pushing its total regional footprint to an unprecedented 98 weekly flights. By entering the US National Capital region, the airline is intentionally establishing a direct pipeline into a market that generates immense corporate, diplomatic, and premium diaspora traffic.

Skipping over traditional multi-stop routings changes the financial landscape for travelers who have spent years navigating arduous connections. In the past, more than 40,000 passengers traveling annually between the Washington metropolitan area and greater Asia had to connect through highly congested West Coast hubs, according to Travel Tourister. The new direct 7,860-mile (12,649 km) sector completely removes that friction, offering an efficient 14-hour eastbound sprint and a 15.5-hour westbound block time, to the delight of these regular travelers.

The economic ramifications of this single-route injection are massive, highlighting the high-yield value of mature transpacific corridors. According to EVA Air, the new service will generate more than $61 million in annual economic impact for the capital region while significantly shifting market-share balances. For an independent carrier operating outside the major transatlantic mega-alliances, getting a firm foothold at Dulles is an excellent way to capture elite corporate accounts that traditionally rely on domestic legacy carriers.

A route is never complete without an accompanying aircraft, and fortunately for EVA Air, the best choice is already in the fleet. The carrier chose to configure its newest batch of Boeing 787-9 Dreamliner airframes in a tightly optimized three-class layout, balancing premium margin and structural density. Total capacity is capped at 278 seats, meticulously divided into 26 Royal Laurel business class suites, 28 Premium Economy spaces, and 224 standard economy seats.

By dedicating just four rows to the premium economy environment in a spacious seven-abreast, 2-3-2 configuration, the airline is breaking firmly with the industry status quo. This cabin density lets the airline offer a generous 42-inch (106.7 cm) seat pitch, giving passengers far more personal space than competitive alternatives. Each seat incorporates an advanced cradle-motion system that slides the lower cushion forward and upward simultaneously, providing an eight-inch (20.3 cm) recline without encroaching on the passenger behind.

The cabin composition maximizes revenue potential per square foot by catering to an affluent middle market that values workspace over basic transit. Each space is loaded with utility, featuring a 15.6-inch (39.6 cm) high-definition touchscreen monitor, built-in personal electronic device holders, and dual power configurations. By choosing a lower seat count over a dense layout, the airline creates a scarce, high-demand asset that lets it maintain strong pricing integrity during both peak and shoulder travel seasons.

One particular carrier is planning to expand its services significantly.

The immediate arrival of a 42-inch (106.7 cm) product standard at Dulles heavily disrupts the traditional long-haul landscape connecting the eastern United States to East Asia. Elite regional competitors have historically maintained a highly uniform product standard on their ultra-long-haul fleets, typically capping their mid-cabin offerings at a conventional 38-inch (96.5 cm) pitch. This four-inch (10.2 cm) discrepancy creates a severe competitive deficit for legacy operators that have long relied on cabin density to maximize raw ticket volume.

Compared with the tight layout choices of alternative alliance carriers, the spatial divergence becomes clear to corporate travelers who pay close attention to every product offering. Competitors operating out of neighboring East Coast hubs often deploy an eight-abreast 2-4-2 alignment on their flagship aircraft, narrowing the cushion width available to individual passengers. Scaling down to a seven-abreast configuration on a widebody frame restores substantial shoulder room, turning a long 15-hour flight track into a manageable workspace.

Put simply, this is precisely how an independent player dictates market expectations from a single international gateway. Travelers experiencing the enhanced spatial layout quickly develop a lower tolerance for restrictive industry-standard cabins on subsequent transpacific journeys. As this route matures into the late summer season, an elite intermediate tier will force surrounding operators to re-evaluate whether their high-density configurations can truly protect their premium market shares.

Launching a direct transpacific service to the East Coast serves a clear structural purpose by shifting traffic away from traditional West Coast connecting gateways. Travelers originating in the mid-Atlantic region had to funnel through immense hubs like Los Angeles or San Francisco to reach East Asia. This extra domestic leg adds hours of travel time, heightens the risk of misplaced baggage, and introduces the fatigue of terminal transfers. Now offering a direct alternative, the carrier skips over these consolidated bottlenecks entirely and establishes a more streamlined transit corridor.

Capturing high-value traffic from the corporate and diplomatic sectors requires direct geographic access to the political heart of the United States. Connecting points like Seattle or Los Angeles add operational friction that premium business travelers actively seek to avoid. The three-class aircraft setup provides dedicated mid-cabin space that aligns with corporate travel rules, which often restrict business class bookings but permit premium travel on long flights, according to a Reddit thread. This means EVA Air now directly competes with legacy alliances by pulling lucrative corporate contracts away from domestic multi-stop routings.

The geographic advantage is particularly striking when analyzing the specific corporate demand from the defense and technology corridors flanking Virginia and Maryland. These fields rely heavily on constant communication with East Asian manufacturing bases, making efficient transpacific transport a vital business requirement, according to the Virginia Economic Development Partnership (VEDP). Avoiding a West Coast transfer saves corporate clients at least three hours each way while ensuring high-yield revenue flows directly to a single carrier instead of being split among domestic codeshare partners.

Operating flights that exceed 15 hours always comes with the steep weight penalties of ultra-long-haul aviation. To sustain a route of this length, an aircraft must carry thousands of gallons of fuel simply to burn it off during the initial hours of flight, which severely restricts the remaining payload capacity available for heavy cargo, according to Aviation Metric. Relying heavily on discounted economy fares cannot offset these operating costs, especially since the maximum size of the business-class cabin is limited by the cabin's dimensions. The route's commercial viability depends heavily on maximizing revenue density in the mid-cabin environment.

The mid-cabin space acts as a financial buffer because it generates premium-tier margins while consuming far less floor space than luxury lie-flat berths. Corporate travelers moving through this cabin category typically pay fares that are twice the cost of a standard economy ticket, yet the footprint per passenger expands by only a fraction. Operating with a highly optimized revenue density like this keeps the route consistently profitable during off-peak travel seasons when leisure demand declines.

The true power of this mid-cabin asset is fully realized when it is integrated into a rapid-transit hub network. Arriving at a hub in the early morning allows travelers to clear transit security and board connecting flights to major East Asian manufacturing and financial centers within a tight window. EVA Air's coordination turns a single ultra-long-haul route into a comprehensive gateway, capturing complex multi-city business itineraries that traditional domestic carriers cannot easily replicate with multi-carrier alliances.

The launch of the Mid-Atlantic corridor is only the initial phase of a broader systematic strategy to standardize this premium configuration across the global network. Introducing a dedicated three-class layout on the twin-engine widebody fleet is, of course, a distinct shift away from treating premium economy as an optional regional product. Ultimately, it positions the mid-cabin experience as a foundational tier that will define future intercontinental operations.

As more factory-fresh airframes arrive from the assembly line, this high-comfort standard will gradually scale into highly competitive West Coast markets and key European trunk routes. Markets like San Francisco, Munich, Milan, and Vienna are scheduled to receive these reconfigured three-class widebodies to ensure product consistency across diverse transpacific and transatlantic flight paths.

EVA Air's fleet harmonization creates an intense competitive challenge for global alliance partners that still operate older configurations with inferior legroom standards. Standardizing an industry-leading pitch across a growing widebody fleet sets a new quality benchmark that leaves rivals with little choice but to invest in costly cabin retrofits or cede lucrative market share. The race is now well and truly on, and it seems that EVA Air is the one to chase.

Tampa Community Turns Out for Sierra Club’s ‘Power & Perspectives: The Energy Dialogue’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

TAMPA, Fla. — On Thursday, November 5, Sierra Club Florida and partner organizations hosted “Power & Perspectives: The Energy Dialogue,” a community town hall addressing TECO bill increases and the need for local utility accountability. Over 25 people attended the event featuring a panel of Sierra Club, Food & Water Watch, and Hillsborough Affordable Energy Coalition representatives, as well as a member of Tampa’s City Council and the city’s Sustainability & Resilience Officer.

TECO has recently boasted rate decreases, however, monthly bills for residential or small business customers using 1,000 kWh per month are still over $10 higher than in December 2024. While families contend with higher bills, the CEO of TECO made $2.5 million last year. The CEO of Emera, TECO’s parent company, made $9.5 million.

“Transparency shouldn’t be optional when families are forced to choose between keeping the lights on and buying groceries,” said Walter Smith II, Sierra Club’s Tampa Bay Organizing Representative. “We invited our neighbors, TECO reps, and environmental justice experts into the same room because the community deserves answers about their energy burdens and the various impacts of fossil fuels on our environment and health. This town hall is a critical first step toward demanding a just, affordable, and clean energy future for everyone in Tampa Bay.”

“Tampa residents have been struggling under the weight of skyrocketing electricity bills, all while Tampa Electric walks away with record profits year after year and passes the costs of fuel, storm recovery, and even its brand new Midtown headquarters onto ratepayers,” said Isabella Moeller, Florida Organizer for Food & Water. “But our community has had enough. TECO is a for-profit company beholden to shareholders only, that’s why we’re making a wide call to our local elected officials to hold TECO and its regulators accountable to the people they serve. Tampa City Council has an opportunity to pass an affordable energy resolution that commits the city to opposing TECO’s unjust rate hikes and supporting clean, affordable energy solutions. We must build on this momentum and ensure that affordable energy is a priority for every elected official in Tampa.”

The Sierra Club is America’s largest and most influential grassroots environmental organization, with millions of members and supporters. In addition to protecting every person's right to get outdoors and access the healing power of nature, the Sierra Club works to promote clean energy, safeguard the health of our communities, protect wildlife, and preserve our remaining wild places through grassroots activism, public education, lobbying, and legal action. For more information, visit www.sierraclub.org.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Ryan Gosling has been incredibly booked and busy of late. He starred in the lead role in the sci-fi smash hit Project Hail Mary earlier this year. In 2027, he'll travel to the galaxy far, far away in Star Wars: Starfighter. Then in 2028 he'll make his long-awaited MCU debut as Ghost Rider, re-teaming with Starfighter director Shawn Levy. If all that wasn't enough, Gosling has lined up one more unexpected project: The Flintstones.

Gosling's production company, Open Invite, and Warner Bros. are in early development on a live-action Flintstones reboot centered on a grown-up Bamm-Bamm, according to Deadline. Currently, Gosling and his Open Invite partner Jessie Henderson are looking for a writer to take on the project. Later this year Open Invite will premiere the romantic drama Love of Your Life at the Toronto International Film Festival. The movie stars Margaret Qualley (The Dog Stars), Aaron Pierre (Lanterns), and Patrick Schwarzenegger (The White Lotus), among others.

Bamm-Bamm first appeared as a baby in the original 1960s Hanna-Barbera cartoon, as the young son of Barney and Betty Rubble. He was depicted as a teenager in The Pebbles and Bamm-Bamm Show in the 1970s, when he started a band with his sister Pebbles. Bamm-Bamm also appeared in the 1994 live-action Flintstones movie.

As Gosling's project is currently in early development, there is no news about who may play Bamm-Bamm or what the story will be. The big question is whether Gosling will be involved in the project beyond his role as a producer. After his widely acclaimed performance as Ken in Greta Gerwig's Barbie, it seems like the actor would be a perfect fit for Bamm-Bamm. Of course, with his schedule already so busy, he may not want to take on yet another project.

There is also the possibility of a Barbie sequel. Warner Bros. would likely prioritize Gosling's return to Barbie over the Flintstones project, since the former is the studio's highest-grossing film.

Here's What to Gift the Maximalist Friend Who Already Has Everything

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A fellow maximalist shares niche beauty, fashion, fragrance, and accessory finds your most extra friend may not know about yet.

Do you have a friend who literally has it all? While shopping for a minimalist has its own struggles, you don't know agony unless you're looking for a gift for the maximalist in your life whose apartment is simply filled with stuff. And I know because I'm that friend (and so is every shopping writer in existence).

Knowing your friend well enough to know exactly what to gift them is good advice in general, but it's especially good advice for the maximalist. You have to know what they already have; that's all on you. As a fellow maximalist, what I can help you with in your gift-shopping journey is to give you recommendations on products that they probably want, but won't buy themselves. We're talking niche, colorful, and unique products across categories that we simply know they'll love and want.

Whether it's a beauty oracle deck your witchy friend who collects tarot decks won't have, or indie fashion and skincare brands they might not know exist (but will definitely be obsessed over), keep scrolling to shop my picks for the best gifts for that maximalist friend on your list.

As a maximalist, your friend would want to change their nails every one or two weeks. So why not gift them press-on nails with detailed designs that look like they just got out of a nail appointment? These nails are some of the best (and longest) you can get, with so many design options available.

Eric Bauza’s Final ‘Looney Tunes’ Performance Is ‘Daffy Season,’ Now in Theaters Ahead of ‘The Cat in the Hat’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The death of Eric Bauza in October shocked the entertainment industry. An impressively prolific voice actor, it would’ve been nearly impossible for anybody engaging with animation over the past 15 years to never come across his work. Bauza’s death came just two months after the delayed release of “Coyote vs. Acme,” the “Looney Tunes” feature once deemed unreleasable by Warner Bros. Discovery and later sold off to Ketchup Entertainment. The film, which grossed $101 million worldwide, is a true showcase for Bauza, who lends his voice to a plethora of iconic characters, including Bugs Bunny, Daffy Duck, Elmer Fudd, Foghorn Leghorn, Tweety, and Porky Pig. Related Stories Box Office: ‘Godzilla Minus Zero’ Roars with $6.7 Million in Previews, ‘Ramayana: Rise of a Legend’ Earns $3.5 Million David Ellison’s Big Bet: Why the Mogul Thinks Skydance Film Chiefs Dana Goldberg and Josh Greenstein Can Deliver 30 Movies a Year

Bauza became something of the defining voice of modern “Looney Tunes” in his final years. Fans can see him give one final performance with the property in “Daffy Season,” a 7-minute animated short that plays before screenings of “The Cat in the Hat,” opening Friday from Skydance’s Warner Bros. Pictures. A representative for the studio confirmed to IndieWire that the short is Bauza’s final “Looney Tunes” movie. Bauza is also credited as providing additional voices for “The Cat in the Hat.”

“Daffy Season” centers on Daffy Duck, voiced by Bauza. He’s joined by Jeff Bergman as Bugs Bunny, Gabriel Iglesias as Speedy Gonzales, and Jeannie Tirado as Lola Bunny. The plot follows Daffy, turning his beak up at his fellow Tunes as they mindlessly obsess over the game of soccer. Todd Wilderman and Hamish Grieve are co-directors. “Daffy Season” is the first theatrically released “Looney Tunes” short since “Daffy’s Rhapsody” played before “Journey 2: The Mysterious Island” back in 2012.

Before “Daffy Season,” Bauza had already provided some voice work for a “Tunes” musical feature titled “Bye Bye Bunny,” which was canceled after some years of development and production. Other “Looney Tunes” credits from Bauza include the HBO Max series “Looney Tunes Cartoons” and the features “The Day the Earth Blew Up: A Looney Tunes Movie” and “Space Jam: A New Legacy.”

Bauza died at his home in Los Angeles on October 25 as a result of hypertensive cardiovascular disease. In a tribute in The Hollywood Reporter, Warner Bros. Pictures Animation producer Susan Rogers credited Bauza with playing a huge role in reviving the “Looney Tunes.”

“There wasn’t a single short he couldn’t quote or couldn’t reference,” she said. “I don’t think you replace Eric Bauza … The hole he leaves is huge. There are many talented people out there, and the franchise will move on. But there’s only one Eric Bauza.”

AC Milan: Diego Moreira is already doing better than Rafael Leão on his debut

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Arriving this summer at AC Milan from Strasbourg for around 60 million euros, bonus included, Diego Moreira already justifies the investment made by the Rossoneri. The winger once again stood out during the derby against Inter, with two assists and a superb free kick to contribute to his team’s victory. A remarkable debut which also allows him to gradually forget Rafael Leão, who left to join Galatasaray.

Moreira’s numbers already speak for him. In five starts in all competitions, the Portuguese has scored four goals and provided three assists. In the league, he has the same record in six appearances, including four in the starting XI. For comparison, during his first season in Serie A in 2019-2020, Rafael Leão needed 24 games to reach the four-goal mark.

Bonnie Wright Shares Update on Newborn Baby's 6 Hospital Visits

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Harry Potter alum Bonnie Wright shared that her and husband Andrew Lococo’s newborn son Aster had been to the hospital six times since his birth at the end of September.

Bonnie Wright has been dealing with a difficult situation.

The Harry Potter alum shared that her and husband Andrew Lococo’s son Aster, who was born on Sept. 28, has been making repeated hospital visits due to an undisclosed health issue.

“Our sweet little Aster has been to the hospital 6 times in his 5 weeks of life,” Wright posted to Instagram Nov. 6 alongside a photo of herself holding the newborn, whose face she covered with a white heart emoji. “Getting treatment for a condition we knew he’d be born with. He is such a strong, chill little baby.”

While the 35-year-old did not share details about Aster’s condition, she did thank friends and family for their help in caring for his 3-year-old brother Elio during this challenging time.

"Feeling so lucky to have such supportive family and friends,” Wright added. “Helping take Elio to preschool, cooking us meals, checking in on us. The village is possible, text/call your pals.”

The Hello Earthling founder and Lococo—who married in 2022—announced Aster’s arrival on Instagram in October, sharing a few snaps of the newborn with his family and his big brother.

“Named after the star shaped flower,” she wrote. “Our little star has been showered in love by his big brother Elio. Thank you universe for my loving bundle of boys!”

Shortly before Aster’s birth, Wright reflected on her final months of pregnancy, especially how different the experience had been compared to her first child.

“This pregnancy has been physically harder,” she wrote in a July post on her Substack. “I’m chasing a toddler, I’m three years older, I’m running on considerably less sleep than I’d like to admit.”

And becoming a mother also gave the former child star a new perspective on her life, admitting she used to believe that she had to land “somewhere definitive” in order to feel like she was gaining clarity.

“Motherhood has slowly, persistently taught me that nothing is actually that finite,” she confessed. “We are always in progress. Sometimes things move forward and sometimes it genuinely feels like a billion steps back and both are just part of it.”

For more stars getting candid about motherhood, keep reading.

The Oscar winner, who has worked with organizations like UNICEF and is a UN Goodwill Ambassador, makes it a priority to share those same humanitarian values with her six children.

"With my family, I'm trying to raise them to have respect for all people and make friends around the world and feel at home with the world and really live a truly global [life]," she told NBC News in 2011. "I think it's what forms them and it's really important to me."

"I know it’s cliché, but I love everything, even the hard parts," the Kardashians star—mom to daughter True and son Tatum with ex Tristan Thompson—said of motherhood in a 2022 interview with Elle. "[My kids] challenge me as a person, and being able to shape little people into really incredible big people is an honor and a gift."

"We have to take those roles seriously, especially in today’s day and age, with how much accessibility children have and the information they’re exposed to so young," she added. "It’s super scary, but I take my job very seriously. I love it so much."

"I have such unconditional love for my kids," the Keep Up With the Kardashians alum—who shares North, Saint, Chicago and Psalm with ex-husband Kanye West—wrote on her website in 2017. "No matter what, I will always love them and support them in anything they choose to do in life."

"My family was so close growing up; now that I'm a mom, I understand the bond my mom and dad felt with us," she continued. "There can be ups and downs with kids but no matter what, I always learn so much from them. Being a mom is the most important job I have."

In a 2018 interview, the Kylie Cosmetics founder shared how much her mindset has changed since giving birth to Stormi that year.

"I think more about the future because of her. Every time I leave and I'm stressed about leaving her, I'm like, ‘I'm doing it for you,'" Kylie—who also shares son Aire with ex Travis Scott—told London Evening Standard. "Everyone says you change completely when you become a mom, but I really feel the same, just better."

The Poosh creator shares kids Mason, Penelope and Reign with ex Scott Disick. She's also mom to son Rocky with husband Travis Barker.

"It's definitely my favorite role in life," she said of motherhood in a 2012 interview with Parents. "It has changed me and made me realize there is nothing else I would rather do with my time. Being a mom is what life is about. I hope people realize what the priorities in life should be and know not everything has to be perfect."

As mom to kids Camden, Jaxon and Saylor with ex Jay Cutler, the Laguna Beach alum told Modeliste Magazine in 2016, "There's just so much more love in your life. I finally feel like I have a real identity"

"I feel like I have a purpose here in life now," she added. "I'm a mom to these three amazing little babies, and my whole world just kind of opened up."

"As soon as I found out I was pregnant," the Pretty Little Liars actress—who shares daughters Atlas and Rome with Matte Babel—said in a 2023 interview with Byrdie, "I put the old version of myself aside, and a new chapter began."

"I never want my children to say I work too much," she continued. "When I'm putting them to bed or having a meal with them, I put my phone down. They're growing up so quickly, and I don't want to look back and think, 'I missed this because of a work function.'"

The Lizzie McGuire alum is mom to son Luca with her ex-husband Mike Comrie. she also shares daughters Banks, Mae and Townes with husband Matthew Koma.

"I don't always balance it all," she admitted of her parenting duties in a 2018 interview with E! News, "I do the best that I can, and some months are crazier than others."

Still, Duff considers herself "a mother first."

"My decision-making process is like, 'Hey can I make this work?'" she said of finding family time in her busy work life. "I've been doing this for a while so I do have a strong work ethic, and I have a lot of people around me that help keep my schedule straight and plan and know where my priorities lie."

The powerhouse singer is a huge inspiration to all of us, but turns out the mother of Blue, Rumi and Sir—who she shares with husband Jay-Z—also makes sure to be the same for her own children.

"It just gives you purpose and all of the things that my self-esteem was associated it, it's all completely different," she said of motherhood in a 2012 interview with Anderson Cooper. "I realized why I was born and more than anything all of the things I want to pass onto my child and the best way of doing that is not by preaching or telling her but showing her by example."

For the Devil Wears Prada star, becoming a mom was a groundbreaking experience in both personal and work life.

“I don't think it's necessary to be a mother to play a mother. I think that should be open to lots of different actors," she told People in 2024, adding that welcoming sons Jonathan and Jack with husband Adam Shulman helped her gain an understanding of "the depth of love" maternal characters can feel.

The Mindy Project actress—who is mom to kids Katherine, Spencer and Anne—said she feels "so much more happy and confident after having children."

"In each subsequent year of my life, I'm just feeling happier and happier," she told ABC News in 2021, "and I am amazed at that,"

Though she played one of the titular Bad Moms in the 2016 comedy, the actress couldn't be a better parent to her kids Wyatt and Dimitri, who she shares with Ashton Kurcher.

"I reprioritized my life, in all honesty," Kunis said of her motherhood journey in a 2016 interview with Entertainment Tonight. "I traveled through all my 20s and I was very selfish—in a good way, not in a way I regret. And I think having a kid made me realize how incredibly selfless I want to be."

The Transformers actress—who shares kids Noah, Bodhi and Journey with her ex-husband Brian Austin Green, as well as daugther Saga with ex-fiancé Machine Gun Kelly—has a lovely approach to her parenting style.

"I don't think I need to teach my children with harsh words or with punishment. I believe that if I love them the right way, there's no need for that," she told Harper's Bazaar Arabia in 2015. "I'm a free-spirit mother."

The Gossip Girl alum and Ryan Reynolds' couple goals include being amazing parents to four children, including all the funny moments that come with being a parent.

"It's tough when you get pooped on and barfed on, but having a baby is wonderful," Lively joked to NW Magazine in 2016. "Even when it's tough and I'm exhausted, I think, 'I am so fortunate.'"

Adam Driver Not Participating in ‘Girls’ Revival: “Wish Them the Best of Luck”

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The two-time Oscar nominee portrayed Adam Sackler, the on-and-off boyfriend of Lena Dunham's Hannah Horvath, in all six seasons of the show.

Adam Driver has confirmed he will not be involved in the upcoming HBO revival of Girls.

In October, it was announced that Lena Dunham, Allison Williams, Jemima Kirke, Zosia Mamet and Andrew Rannells are all set to return for the limited five-episode reboot of the series. Left off the confirmed list of castmembers coming back was Driver, who appeared in all six seasons of Girls.

When asked by CinemaBlend in an interview published Friday if viewers should expect him to reprise his Girls character, he said, “No, but I wish them the best of luck.” Related Stories Movies Adam Driver Says "the Ship Has Sailed" on Playing Kylo Ren: "No More 'Star Wars'" Movies Adam Driver Confirms 'Heat 2' Villain Role, Shares Michael Mann Update After Accidental Shooting

The two-time Oscar nominee portrayed Adam Sackler, the on-and-off boyfriend of Dunham’s Hannah Horvath. Dunham, who created, wrote and starred in the beloved comedy-drama, recounted Driver’s alleged temper on set of the show in her 2026 memoir Famesick, and further alleged that he threw a chair at a wall near her.

“At the time, I didn’t have the skill to … it never entered my mind to say, ‘I am your boss, you can’t speak to me this way,’” Dunham said in an April interview with The Guardian about the actor. “And, at that point in my 20s, I still thought that’s what great male geniuses do: eviscerate you. Which is weird, because I was raised by a male genius who would never do that.”

Dunham will executive produce the Girls reboot with Judd Apatow (who was an EP on the original) and Ilene S. Landress and Michael P. Cohen of Good Thing Going. Williams, Kirke, Mamet and Rannells are co-EPs. Production begins in 2027.

Saints to have "chip on our shoulder" after Browns' "chirping"

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Saints, like all teams, pay attention to the things said by their upcoming opponents.

Earlier this week, Browns quarterback Shedeur Sanders seemed to make some less-than-flattering comments about the New Orleans defense. The New Orleans defense heard it, loud and clear.

“We’re gonna come in with a chip on our shoulder, for sure,” Saints defensive end Carl Granderson said Friday. “You know, they been chirping a little bit. We heard what the quarterback . . . said. So, you know, we’re [going to] come in hot and ready to play. . . . It’s gonna be a good game, for sure. We’re gonna come out with the [win].”

Asked Wednesday by reporters whether he has had a chance to study the Saints defense and to explain what he has seen, Sanders flashed a broad smile.

“Yeah, I’ve seen a lot,” Sanders said. “When I smile, just know that means something. I’m maturing. I’m maturing. Like, I could have given a decent answer, but now I’m just going to smile whenever it’s just, like, some questions.”

The better approach for Sanders would have been to project a poker face, and to give an answer that did nothing to attract the attention of the Saints’ defense.

So while he says he’s maturing, the ultimate level of maturity would consist of providing the most vanilla answer possible, praising the Saints and explaining that it will be a major challenge to beat them.

Instead, a Browns player has provided the next opponent — for the second straight week — with a little extra incentive.
